## 3 KEEP-Bench

## 3.1 Task Formulation

Given a query q and a source context c, a compressor produces a compressed context c˜ from c. KEEP-Bench evaluates evidence preservation by assessing whether c˜ faithfully preserves all evidence in c required to carry out the <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>eman</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> $q .$ F<sub>or eac</sub>h i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance, we represen</sub>t thi<sub>s ev</sub>id<sub>ence as a se</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence propos</sub>iti<sub>ons,</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> $\mathcal { E } _ { q } ( c ) = \{ p _ { 1 } , . . . , p _ { n } \}$ <sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h $p _ { i }$ i<sub>s se</sub>lf<sub>-con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>, a</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s</sub> preservation in c˜ to be assessed independently of the others.

A <sub>compresse</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t i<sub>s cons</sub>id<sub>ere</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>ithf<sub>u</sub>l if <sub>every ev</sub>i<sub>-</sub> dence proposition is preserved. An evidence proposition p<sub>i</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>cons</sub>id<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>preserve</sub>d if it i<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n,</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>unam</sub>bi<sub>guous</sub>l<sub>y</sub> inferable from, c˜ alone. If c˜ omits $p _ { i }$ <sub>or s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>ncons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith it<sub>,</sub> $p _ { i }$ i<sub>s cons</sub>id<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>no</sub>t <sub>preserve</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

## 4.1 Experimental Setup

Compressors. We evaluated both dedicated compressors <sub>an</sub>d <sub>genera</sub>l LLM<sub>s.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>compressors</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> extractive methods EXIT (Hwan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2025) and RE-COMP (extractive) (Xu Shi and Choi 2024) and the abstractive methods BRIEF-Pro (Gu et al. 2026) and RECOMP (abstractive). General LLMs included Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-14B, and Qwen3-32B (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2025); Gemma-4- E2B<sub>-</sub>it<sub>,</sub> G<sub>emma-</sub>4<sub>-</sub>E4B<sub>-</sub>it<sub>,</sub> G<sub>emma-</sub>4<sub>-</sub>12B<sub>-</sub>it<sub>, an</sub>d G<sub>emma-</sub>4<sub>-</sub> 31B-it (Gemma Team 2026); Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct, Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (Grattafiori et al. 2024); and the two <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>rietar<sub>y</sub> models GPT-5.4-mini (O<sub>p</sub>enAI 2026) and Claude-Haiku-4.5 (Anthro<sub>p</sub>ic 2025).

Separating parallel evidence reduces joint preservation. Objective. Parallel evidence. To construct a parallel instance, we replace one or more key entities in the query with underspecified fictional mentions. For an underspecified mention r, we introduce two explicit referents $r _ { 1 }$ <sub>an</sub>d $r _ { 2 }$ <sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>re</sub>f<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i<sub>pa</sub>t<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>a separa</sub>t<sub>e reason</sub>i<sub>ng c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n w</sub>ith th<sub>e same num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f h<sub>ops an</sub>d th<sub>e same</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e query,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns use</sub> disjoint referents, intermediate entities, and supporting evidence. They also terminate in distinct valid answers.

Domain-specific finalization. Science cases contain three or four distinct explanatory perspectives supported by scholarly <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Wh<sub>en re</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>eva</sub>l <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>es su</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>ve suppor</sub>t f<sub>or severa</sub>l <sub>perspec</sub>ti<sub>ves,</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>nan</sub>t<sub>–m</sub>i<sub>nor</sub>it<sub>y con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub> tions can be instantiated from the same underlying question. ## B.2 Quality Control and Filtering

Qualit<sub>y</sub> control was inte<sub>g</sub>rated throu<sub>g</sub>hout data construction. Althou<sub>g</sub>h LLMs assisted with candidate <sub>g</sub>eneration and rewritin<sub>g</sub>, <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances were manua</sub>ll<sub>y rev</sub>i<sub>ewe</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>e</sub>dit<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en necessary.</sub> N<sub>o propos</sub>iti<sub>on, en</sub>tit<sub>y mapp</sub>i<sub>ng, or suppor</sub>ti<sub>ng passage</sub> <sub>was accep</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as a go</sub>ld <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

General validation criteria. Every target proposition had to be self-contained, supported by an identifiable part of the context, <sub>an</sub>d <sub>necessary</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> Th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e propos</sub>iti<sub>on se</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> h<sub>a</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e su</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>o o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e go</sub>ld <sub>answer or answer aspec</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e remove</sub>d <sub>propos</sub>iti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>were over</sub>l<sub>y</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>, re</sub>d<sub>un</sub>d<sub>an</sub>t<sub>, unsuppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, or</sub> d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>on ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge, an</sub>d <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances w</sub>ith i<sub>ncomp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e reason</sub>i<sub>ng c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns,</sub> i<sub>ncons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons, or un</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>cu</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

Ifneutrinos are purely Diracfermions, their small masses can be attributed to extremely small Yukawa couplings to the Higgs field. In the seesaw setting, the mass Lagrangian contains this Dirac term together with a heavy right-handed Majorana contribution, so that the resulting Dirac-Majorana structure underlies a seesaw mechanism of neutrino mass <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sma</sub>ll <sub>masses</sub> <sub>o</sub>f k<sub>nown</sub> <sub>neu</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>nos</sub> b<sub>y</sub> <sub>assum</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>arge</sub> <sub>masses</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>t<sub>er</sub>il<sub>e</sub> <sub>neu</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>nos.</sub> <sub>.</sub> <sub>.</sub> <sub>.</sub>

. . . The matrix has several important limits. When the Dirac term dominates while the Majorana entries are tiny, the neutrinosform pseudo-Dirac pairs whose small mass splittings are generated by tiny lepton-number-violating terms. . . .

Evidence propositions and gold answer aspects.

• Dominant: Majorana masses <sub>g</sub>enerated throu<sub>g</sub>h a seesaw mechanism.

• Minority: Dirac masses from extremel<sub>y</sub> small Yukawa cou<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub>s.

• Minority: Pseudo-Dirac masses with tin<sub>y</sub> le<sub>p</sub>ton-number violation.

• Minority: Radiative masses <sub>g</sub>enerated throu<sub>g</sub>h loo<sub>p</sub>-level new <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sics.

Evidence propositions and gold answer aspects.

• Dominant: In the Euro<sub>p</sub>ean Union<sub>,</sub> online or distance <sub>p</sub>urchases normall<sub>y</sub> have a 14-da<sub>y</sub> withdrawal <sub>p</sub>eriod from deliver<sub>y,</sub> with exce<sub>p</sub>t<sup>i</sup>ons.

• Dominant: In China<sub>,</sub> eli<sub>g</sub>ible online <sub>p</sub>urchases can use a 7-da<sub>y</sub> no-reason return rule<sub>,</sub> with listed exce<sub>p</sub>tions.

• Dominant: In Ja<sub>p</sub>an<sub>,</sub> mail-order or ordinar<sub>y</sub> online sales are not covered b<sub>y</sub> coolin<sub>g</sub>-of<sub>;</sub> if no return terms are shown<sub>,</sub> an 8-da<sub>y</sub> <sup>ret</sup>u<sup>rn</sup> <sup>ro</sup>u<sup>te</sup> <sup>ma</sup>y <sup>a</sup>pp<sup>l</sup>y w<sup>ith</sup> <sup>cons</sup>u<sup>mer-</sup>p<sup>aid</sup> p<sup>osta</sup>g<sup>e</sup>.

• Minority: In Australia<sub>,</sub> ordinar<sub>y</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>e-of-mind returns are not an automatic statutor<sub>y</sub> ri<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>;</sub> consumer-<sub>g</sub>uarantee remedies a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t h<sub>as a pro</sub>bl<sub>em.</sub>

## C.2 Compressor Configuration

All compressors except RECOMP (abstractive) received a sentence budget K. Under the default budget, K = 2h for paralle and conditional cases with h hops and $K = 2 n + 1$ for imbalanced cases with n perspectives, where the extra sentence leaves room to state that the <sub>q</sub>uestion is o<sub>p</sub>en-ended. The 0.5× and 2× bud<sub>g</sub>ets in Section 5.2 halved or doubled the ho<sub>p</sub> or <sub>p</sub>ers<sub>p</sub>ective term. RECOMP (abstractive) has no len<sub>g</sub>th control and was run once with its default settin<sub>g</sub>s.

G<sub>enera</sub>l LLM<sub>s rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>a sys</sub>t<sub>em</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> ICL d<sub>emons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ons, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>user message con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e ques</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> original context, and K. The demonstrations were built with the same pipeline as the benchmark and excluded from evaluation. Each demonstration followed the budget of its own case, so one prompt could contain several values of K, while the final <sub>message s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>case.</sub> Thi<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng was</sub> di<sub>sa</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> O<sub>pen-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s ran on v</sub>LLM i<sub>n</sub> bfl<sub>oa</sub>t16 <sub>w</sub>ith t<sub>empera</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> 0 <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t <sub>mos</sub>t 65<sub>,</sub>536 <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens;</sub> th<sub>e propr</sub>i<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ary mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s use</sub>d t<sub>empera</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> 0<sub>, a</sub>t <sub>mos</sub>t 1<sub>,</sub>024 <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens, an</sub>d <sub>no</sub> thi<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng or reason</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> Th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e promp</sub>t<sub>s are prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> E<sub>.</sub>2<sub>.</sub>

The dedicated com<sub>p</sub>ressors used their released check<sub>p</sub>oints. RECOMP (extractive) and EXIT ke<sub>p</sub>t the K hi<sub>g</sub>hest scorin<sub>g</sub> sentences, with EXIT scoring each sentence within its own document, and BRIEF-Pro received K through its standard prompt. RECOMP (abstractive), trained on in<sub>p</sub>uts of 512 tokens, received u<sub>p</sub> to 8,192 in<sub>p</sub>ut tokens so that ever com<sub>p</sub>lete context fit.

## C.4 Inference FLOPs Estimation

We estimated major matrix multiplication FLOPs from each method’s public architecture using its actual sequence lengths. Each multiply-add counts as two FLOPs. We included attention and feed-forward projections, attention matrix multiplications, cross-attention where applicable, and vocabulary projections. We assumed KV caching and excluded non-matrix operations, batching, padding, preprocessing, and judge computation. These estimates represent architectural computation rather than l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ency or energy consump</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> P<sub>ropr</sub>i<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ary</sub> API <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s were exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

Decoder-only compressors. Let P and G denote the complete prompt length and compressed output length under the <sub>correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub>i<sub>zer.</sub> With KV <sub>cac</sub>hi<sub>ng,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> T<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ormer processes</sub>

$$
T = P + G - 1\tag{1}
$$

positions, while the vocabulary projection is evaluated at $Z = G$ <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ons.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>lidi<sub>ng-w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ith window w, we define

$$
\mathcal { A } _ { \infty } ( T ) = \frac { T ( T + 1 ) } { 2 } ,\tag{2}
$$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { A _ { w } ( T ) = \left\{ \begin{array} { l l } { A _ { \infty } ( T ) , } & { T \leq w , } \\ { w T - \displaystyle \frac { w ( w - 1 ) } { 2 } , } & { T > w . } \end{array} \right. } \end{array}\tag{3}
$$

Qwen3, Llama, and BRIEF-Pro follow $F _ { m } ( T , Z ) = a _ { m } T + b _ { m } A _ { \infty } ( T ) + c _ { m } Z ,$ <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> G<sub>emma-</sub>4 <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>es</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l

$$

Prefix caching. For each open-weight LLM, we constructed one prefix cache for each of the parallel, conditional, imbalanced <sub>sc</sub>i<sub>ence,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>m</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $p _ { m , s }$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>eng</sub>th <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>onges</sub>t <sub>common</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> $n _ { s }$ <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d prompts in subset s. For case i, the prefix-amortized computation is

$$

$$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { F _ { \mathrm { R E C O M P - a b s } } = 7 0 4 , 6 4 3 , 0 7 2 n + 9 8 , 3 0 4 n ^ { 2 } + 7 7 0 , 4 4 1 , 2 1 6 r } \\ & { ~ + ~ 9 8 , 3 0 4 \left( \mathcal { A } _ { \infty } ( r ) + n r \right) , } \end{array}\tag{9}

$$

# D Configuration and Validation of LLM-as-Judge

## D.1 Judge Configuration

We used GPT-5.2 as the automatic judge, with reasoning efort set to high. Each compressor output was evaluated independently against the corresponding target evidence propositions. We used one model response per judgement, without repeated sampling or majority voting. Parameters not explicitly specified in our implementation used the API defaults.

## D.2 Decision Procedure

For the parallel setting, the judge received the compressed context and one target proposition. When a proposition involved an ambiguous entity, we additionally provided metadata identifying the competing unambiguous entities. The judge then accepted th<sub>e propos</sub>iti<sub>on on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> if th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>emen</sub>t <sub>suppor</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> it <sub>was</sub> ti<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e correc</sub>t <sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y</sub>’<sub>s</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>hi<sub>ng qua</sub>lifi<sub>er or</sub> <sub>an unam</sub>bi<sub>guous equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e exac</sub>t l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>was no</sub>t <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>cou</sub>ld <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> k<sub>eep</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ves apar</sub>t<sub>, eac</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ith it<sub>s own qua</sub>lifi<sub>er, prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>one were no</sub>t <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>th<sub>er.</sub> E<sub>v</sub>id<sub>ence a</sub>b<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>ano</sub>th<sub>er</sub> li<sub>s</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y, or ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>are am</sub>bi<sub>guous name, cou</sub>ld th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore no</sub>t <sub>suppor</sub>t th<sub>e propos</sub>iti<sub>on.</sub>

Th<sub>e con</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>se</sub>tti<sub>ng use</sub>d t<sub>wo eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ures.</sub> O<sub>r</sub>di<sub>nary propos</sub>iti<sub>ons were eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e genera</sub>l proposition-level entailment prompt. For propositions governed by an explicit condition, the judge jointly returned condition\_judgement and overall\_judgement. We used condition\_judgement as the <sub>p</sub>reservation label for the condition-bearin ro osition and overall\_judgement as the label for the associated eneral ro osition. The latte was evaluated under the sco<sub>p</sub>e im<sub>p</sub>osed b<sub>y</sub> the condition and had to be no when the condition itself was not established b<sub>y</sub> the <sub>compresse</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e accompany</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> di<sub>sam</sub>bi<sub>gua</sub>ti<sub>on propos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>er am</sub>bi<sub>gu</sub>it<sub>y reso</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub> and helps the judge interpret its scope; it is not itself an evaluated proposition.

For the science and policy subsets of the imbalanced setting, the judge received the benchmark question, the compressed <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>one</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve.</sub> It d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>compresse</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>preserve</sub>d th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>answer</sub> t<sub>o</sub> the question. For policy perspectives, the compressed context also had to identify the jurisdiction to which the answer applies.

All model res<sub>p</sub>onses were re<sub>q</sub>uested in JSON format. The standard <sub>p</sub>rocedures returned {"judgement":"yes"} or {"judgement":"no"}, whereas the condition-aware <sub>p</sub>rocedure returned both condition\_judgement and overall\_judgement. Failed or un<sub>p</sub>arseable re<sub>q</sub>uests were retried; em<sub>p</sub>t<sub>y</sub> out<sub>p</sub>uts and, in the imbalanced settin<sub>g</sub>, the placeholder output “This passage doesn’t contain relevant information to the question.” were labeled no without a judge call. Th<sub>ese</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s were aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs o</sub>f S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>1<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e promp</sub>t<sub>s are g</sub>i<sub>ven</sub> i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> E<sub>.</sub>3<sub>.</sub>

## D.3 Automatic Judge Validation

<table><tr><td>Setting</td><td>Decisions</td><td>Exact Matches</td><td>Agreement</td></tr><tr><td>Parallel</td><td>100</td><td>95</td><td>95.0%</td></tr><tr><td>Conditional</td><td>100</td><td>91</td><td>91.0%</td></tr><tr><td>Imbalanced</td><td>100</td><td>93</td><td>93.0%</td></tr><tr><td>Overall</td><td>300</td><td>279</td><td>93.0%</td></tr></table>

Table 11: Decision-level exact agreement between the automatic judge and manual validation judgements.

To assess the reliability of the automatic judge, we manually validated 100 sampled proposition-level decisions from each evidence setting, for a total of 300 decisions. Each decision corresponds to one judge call for one target proposition; conditionaware calls in the conditional settin<sub>g</sub> also assess the accom<sub>p</sub>an<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> condition and return both condition\_judgement and overall\_judgement. The samples were stratified by the judge’s output to cover both positive and negative decisions.

For the parallel setting, we sampled 50 decisions judged as yes and 50 judged as no. For the imbalanced setting, the sample contained 50 science and 50 policy decisions, while also comprising 50 yes and 50 no judgements. For the conditional settin<sub>g</sub>, we sam<sub>p</sub>led 20 decisions from each of the followin<sub>g</sub> five out<sub>p</sub>ut cate<sub>g</sub>ories: <sub>g</sub>eneral judgement=yes; <sub>g</sub>eneral judgement=no; condition\_judgement=no with overall\_judgement=no; condition\_judgement=yes with overall\_judgement=yes; and condition\_judgement=yes with overall\_judgement=no.

We measured decision-level exact agreement between the automatic and manual judgements. For outputs containing a single judgement field, the decision was counted as an agreement only when that field matched the manual judgement. For condition-aware out<sub>p</sub>uts, both condition\_judgement and overall\_judgement had to match their res<sub>p</sub>ective manual jud ements; a mismatch in either field caused the entire decision to be counted as a disa reement.

As shown in Table 11, the automatic judge achieves at least 91.0% exact agreement in every setting and 93.0% overall.

<table><tr><td>Evaluation: Parallel Evidence</td></tr><tr><td>System prompt.</td></tr><tr><td>You are a precise JSON editor. Output only JSON that conforms to the schema.</td></tr><tr><td>User prompt.</td></tr><tr><td>You are given a JSON input with two keys: &quot;context &quot; and &quot;proposition&quot;.</td></tr><tr><td>Your task is to determine whether the proposition is entailed by the context.</td></tr><tr><td>Entailment means that the proposition can be logically inferred from at least one coherent part of the context.</td></tr><tr><td>Important clarifications:</td></tr><tr><td>• The context may contain irrelevant, noisy, or even contradictory information. This does NOT invalidate entailment. As long as there exists at least one subset of the context from which the proposition can be reasonably inferred, the proposition should be considered entailed.</td></tr><tr><td>• The proposition does not need to match the wording used in the context. As long as the proposition holds under a reasonable interpretation of some part of the context, it should be considered entailed.</td></tr><tr><td>If the context entails the proposition, output exactly:</td></tr><tr><td>{&quot;judgement&quot;:&quot;yes&quot;}</td></tr><tr><td>Otherwise, output exactly:</td></tr><tr><td>{&quot;judgement&quot;:&quot;no&quot;}</td></tr><tr><td>Do not include any explanation or extra text. Output JSON only.</td></tr><tr><td>Additional Disambiguation Constraints (all must be applied):</td></tr><tr><td>{{CONSTRAINTS_JSON}}</td></tr><tr><td>Rules for judgement (apply to every constraint listed above):</td></tr><tr><td>• Each listed &quot;unambiguous entity &quot; consists of the ambiguous name plus a distinguishing qualifier (for example a country, a county, a date, a type, or an associated person or work). The constraint is about identifying the correct entity. It does not require the label&#x27;s exact wording or its parenthetical form, and it does not require every part of the qualifier to be reproduced, as long as what appears is enough to single out that entity rather than the other listed alternatives.</td></tr><tr><td>• If the proposition mentions one of the listed &quot;unambiguous entities&quot;, then context can be judged as entailment only if the proposition-relevant statement in context is tied to that same entity, because that entity&#x27;s distinguishing qualifier, or an unambiguous equivalent of it, appears in or is attached to that statement.</td></tr><tr><td>• If the proposition-relevant statement carries only the bare ambiguous name, with no distinguishing qualifier attached to it, the judgement must be &quot;no&quot;, even if that qualifier appears elsewhere in the context in connection with something else.</td></tr><tr><td>• If the proposition-relevant statement is tied to a different listed entity than the one the proposition mentions, the judgement must be &quot;no&quot; even if other content appears compatible.</td></tr><tr><td>• The context may instead keep the alternatives apart, attaching each one to its own distinguishing qualifier (for example “X is a town in County A, and X is also a place in County B&quot;). This satisfies the constraint for whichever alternative the proposition mentions, provided that alternative&#x27;s facts are not attributed to the other.</td></tr><tr><td>• If several constraints are listed, the judgement must be &quot;no&quot; unless every constraint whose entities the proposition mentions is satisfied.</td></tr><tr><td>• These are necessary conditions only; all original entailment requirements must still be satisfied.</td></tr><tr><td>#### Input</td></tr><tr><td>{{INPUT_JSON}}</td></tr><tr><td>#### Output</td></tr></table>

## Important clarifications:
